tbmm-2022-06-15sit03spe0242par0092	Congressmans, I'd like to briefly explain the opinion of our presidency regarding the opposition to the Constitutional Opposition of the Proposal. As it is known, according to Article 38 of the Civil Code, the Commission is obliged to first examine whether the proposals referred to to it are contrary to the text and the spirit of the Constitution. The Commission must reject it before it violates the terms of the proposal it considers unconstitutional. The Health, Family, Work and Social Affairs Commission and the Plan and Budget Commission, which consider the proposal as the main commission, considered it unconstitutional, discussed the proposal and referred its report to our presidency and took its place on our agenda in 337 rows. It is possible for the General Assembly to present claims of unconstitutionalism during talks on the entire proposal. The General Assembly has the authority to reject the adoption of articles after such opinions by serious view of claims against the Constitution. Again, the motions to reject a specific clause during the meeting of the General Assembly are voted before other proposals, according to Article 84 of the Civil Code. As you can see, there are a number of rules in both the commission and the General Assembly that allow the proposal to be rejected if the proposal is considered unconstitutional. For these reasons, I feel that it is not in accordance with the Civil Code to discuss claims of violation of the Constitution before starting negotiations with the proposal. On the grounds that I have explained, there is no change in the attitude of starting negotiations with the proposal. It's for the information of the General Assembly.
